## Version 2.1.0 (2026-10-16; the results those of 2.0.0 on every shipped world and the paper's run; a level above 6,949 is refused), the tag v2.1.0, Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.23421502

The step is Rule3 in the law's flux form (the owner's word of 2026-10-16: the engine matches the law in its implementation, not only in its results). Against 2.0.0:

- **The step.** `core/rule3.rule3` is the law's line, w (a_next - 2 a_now + a_before) + r' - r = SUM over the six Links of R_ij (m_ij - a_now) - D_i a_now, with the law's names (the reach R_ij per Link, the mass D_i = 12 (den - num) p_0^2 G^2 per Node, `rule3.mass`, the wall w, the remainder r; the own weight S_i = 2 w - D_i - SUM R_ij derived for the form's Node term and the click's face), the one carried division under it `rule3.carried_division`; the step's module `src/event_universe/flux.py` gathers the Links' sum Link by Link (`link_fluxes`: one difference per Link, each end's own reach; the walls Links to the fill; an axis of one layer a self-Link of flux 0), a turned or folded plane by the one complex line (`turned_link_fluxes`, the neighbour's pair turned by the Link's pair, the far end the conjugate pair, the folded axis's odd parts cancelling), the click's face as the value in the place of one arrival (`face_differences`); `node.step`, `node.step_family`, `node.step_records` and `plane.step_plane` are gone from `src/`, the law's first form (SUM R_ij m_ij + S a_now - w a_before + r) standing in `tests/laws.py` and `tools/step_oracle.py` as the step's oracle. The lattice's loop, the held write, the twist, the lays, the growth, the detector's acts and the phase act are unchanged.
- **The names.** Gone from `src/`: `node.step`, `node.step_family`, `node.step_records`, `plane.step_plane`, `plane.Rule` (now `core/rule3.Rule`, a NamedTuple (reaches, self_coefficient, wall, mass) from `rule3.rule_integers`, which `node.rule_of` returns), `flux.Peaks`, `flux.peaks_of`, `flux.divided`, `loader/derived.PHASE_LINE`, and node.py's re-exports of records.py and readings.py, `axis_neighbours`, `axis_sources_of`, `currents_of`, `momentum_of`, `ports`, `sense_current_of`, `stresses_of`, `Families`, `NodeState`, `Record`, `Rulers`, `Sourcing`, `States`, `empty_record`, `empty_state`, `form`, `full`, `largest`, `level_at`, `phase_lines`, `record_slice`, `row_levels`, `rows_total`, `rulers_write_factor`, `well`, `write_origins`, `write_sources`, `wronskian`, `zeros`, each read from its own module now. Changed: `features/click.presented(arrived, rule, now, other, carry, faces, offset, direction)` takes the `Rule` in place of (reads, S, w) and forms the Node's total by `rule3.flux_total`; `rule3.partial_rooms(reaches, self_coefficient, wall, mass, links, arrivals)`; `rule3.wall_of(den, gamma, unit)` new.
- **The cost.** Measured per interval, the median of a run on one host (pristine 2.0.0 -> 2.1.0): the time paper's run (400x8x8, 172 intervals) 45.8 -> 51.3 ms, +12 percent; pixel (25x25x25, 100 intervals) 81.5 -> 69.2 ms, -15 percent; the resident memory unchanged (59 -> 60 MB, 57 -> 57 MB).
- **The oracle.** `tests/test_step_oracle.py`: on every example world and the time paper's run the engine's step equals the first form bit for bit at every interval compared (every line's two levels and remainder, every write remainder, every phase line, the output lines; positronium_toy 30 of its 1,000 intervals, shelved_ion and shelved_ion_control 1,000 of 96,000 each, pixel 100 of 400, the zeno_pulsed worlds 200 of 768, every other world and the paper's run all of their intervals) and runs back with the record to the lay exactly; `tools/step_oracle.py` holds the first form and the comparison, the suite runs it on two worlds under thirty seconds.
- **The amplitude bound.** The engine's and no line of the law: `World.amplitude_bound` is now the largest level at which every partial sum of the flux line, added in the order the code adds them (w (2 a_now - a_before), - D_i a_now, + the Links' sum, + r), stays inside the width (`derived.bound_under_rooms`, the five quotients; `rule3.rule_total_bound` the same five as one function; ENGINE.md, The amplitude bound): 6,949 at the shipped universes at G = 16 (9,266 under the first form's total), 24,710 at pair.json's Link unit 8 with its turned planes. A behaviour change named: a world whose levels pass 6,949 is now refused (none of the shipped worlds reaches it: the largest level over every shipped world and the paper's run, the two shelved_ion worlds over their first 2,000 of 96,000 intervals, is 3,003; `tools/peaks.py` measures the levels and the step's partial sums beside a run), and the resonant act's scale `resonance.scale_of`, derived from A, rises by one power of two at the windows 60 and 32 and at the detectors' longest windows of 9 shipped worlds (the ghz worlds, neutron_conversion, two_slits, which_way, one_gap, two_gaps), whose records were checked unchanged on all nine.
- **The readings.** `readings.linear_sum`, `linear_exchange`, `weighted_linear_sum` (the flux form's linear sum L, its exchange through the faces and its pace-weighted form) and `tools/linear_sum.py`, with `tests/test_the_flux_form.py` (the identity at every Node of four small worlds, L's change by the named terms); the step's four partial sums (the free term, the Node's own terms, the Links' sum, the whole total) measured beside a run by `tools/peaks.py`, outside the step, their largest sizes over a world.
- **The documents.** ENGINE.md: the step in flux form, the naming table (every symbol of Rule3's flux line against its identifier), the amplitude bound as the engine's promise with its five partial sums and the number 6,949; ALGEBRA.md: the direction's clause and `### The bound` say the law carries no bound and the engine derives its A, and five new paragraphs, The tick is the count of rounds; The remainders' walk accumulates on a bounded board; The flux form of Rule3; The flux form on a turned or folded Link; Gravity's flux and the enclosed write; HIGHLIGHTS.md, CONTRIBUTING.md and llms.txt say the step is Rule3 in flux form.

## Version 2.0.0 (2026-10-11), the tag v2.0.0, Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.23289515

The engine V2, on main: Rule3 only. Against 1.1.0, by theme.

- **The rule and its acts.** Every act on a level is Rule3's one carried division with the remainder kept; everything else is a coefficient's one division with the remainder discarded or a search by comparing integer products. No shear, no rotation act, no sine or cosine, no root, no float, no table: `features/rotation` is deleted, `division_fixed_point` is deleted, and the one search is `core/rule3.largest_below`, the largest integer whose square is at most a count, found by bisection on the share reading (`src/event_universe/core/rule3.py`, `resonance.py`, `emission.py`, `loader/derived.py`, `tools/pixel_mode.py`).
- **The families' phase and gravity, the fold form.** The sign's Link phase and gravity's odd lines ride one fold: each Link's read is a pair (X^c, X^s) in the Link unit with one rounding per part. The time turn is replaced by the phase line, a Rule3 pair at one fixed angle iterated by a level (`features/phase`, the NodeState's `phases`, `node.phased`, `plane.link_pairs`, `plane.sign_pairs`), and the Link's count carries each end's clock as one coefficient rounding (`core/paces.turn_factor`). The amplitude bounds under the fold: `examples/events/turning.json` 7,591 (5,961 at the tag), `pair_atom` 29,482 (23,302 at the tag), `charged.json` 9,266 unchanged.
- **The click and its momentum.** Rule3 conserves a lattice momentum exactly in the rationals at uniform paces, to a bound each interval on the integers, a theorem of the law (ALGEBRA.md, The lattice momentum). The piece's momentum is read at the detector and booked, and at a click the taker is given the piece's momentum as far as it can hold it, the rest booked, by a twist of its lines found by bisection on the phase line to the quarter turn's cap, the inverse through the click exact; the click has two receivers, the taker's part folded by +p_a and the piece's source's part by -p_a at the click's tick by one twist, the source drawn among the emitters by the quanta each still holds, the file's lay an entry in that draw by its laid count (`twist.py`, the click's momentum and its items, `meeting.py`, `credit.py`, `node_detector.py`, `bookings.py`, `features/click`, `features/currents`).
- **The window's turn.** A record declared a NodeDetector at one Node reads its window's turn by carried divisions on the labels, with the hop pair from the phase line (`resonance.window_pair`, `resonance.hopped`); no root at the close.
- **The write and the wiping.** The null window's re-lay keeps each Node's own direction, so the fold survives a body's null close; a region that takes folds no source and books "P lost to the region" and "unpaid by the region"; a click's write at a count above 1 lays the entered part in one direction and wipes the part's earlier gradient, an open item by name (`meeting.py`, `lattice.py`, `node.py`).
- **The remainder's carry.** Every act on a level keeps its remainder between intervals: Rule3's step, the phase pair, the window pair, the held write and the write carried forward and back, the twist. The acts table names every carried act, the step's and the ones outside it, and every division of `src/event_universe` with its kind: 202 call sites under 129 keys, R carried on levels (19), D a coefficient's division once (86), S a search by comparison (24); a new division anywhere fails the table until it is listed (`tests/test_the_acts_table.py`).
- **The report's record and the credit line.** The credit line carries six new keys, momentum, fan, twist and lost from the write, source and recoil from the two receivers; the two slits' output equals the tag's oracle bit for bit with the six keys stripped (`credit.py`, `reports.py`, `records.py`, `features/read`).
- **The modules.** The engine is nineteen modules: the click's momentum and its items stand in `twist.py` (the twist, the fold, the two receivers, the re-lay) beside `meeting.py`, the readings of a record's lines in `readings.py` beside `node.py`, which re-exports them, the rotation unit K = omega_0 Gamma in `features/phase` (`phase.rotation_unit`, a feature importing core alone) and the turned plane's room in `plane.py` (`plane.turned_room`); every module within the shape's limits or at its count of the tag.
- **The tests and the gates.** The suite grows from 150 tests at the tag to 212, the new files `tests/test_part_a_fold.py` to `tests/test_part_h_fix.py`, `tests/test_the_phase_line.py`, `tests/test_the_acts_table.py` and the gates' additions in `tests/test_the_gates.py` and `tests/test_rule3.py`. Three gates are the contributor's law and run on every test run: no root anywhere in src, no shear, no rotation act and no trigonometric call, and the acts table. The documents' three gates on the law and the engine's document (the history clauses, the numbers table, the derivation scripts) run unchanged. The tests' oracles stand under `tests/oracle/`: the two slits' look file of the tag's engine (`tools/look/record.py` over 130 intervals, compressed), the Coulomb run's record before the phase line and the one photon's output before the two receivers, each read bit for bit by its test.
- **The documents.** ENGINE.md describes the engine as main holds it, its section 10 the known issues of the tag (the shelved ion at z = 7 with its refusal at 18,099, the Zeno worlds not re-run at the frozen hash, the holders' swing under a body laid off the step's fixed point) and the engine V2's open items by name: the two-clock closure's integer, emitters whose light leaves the board, the fan's remainder off-centre, the per-source share, the one-Node write, the dipole, the front's delay, the region's +p, a click's write at a count above 1, and `examples/events/shelved_ion` refused by the amplitude bound. The law carries the click's algebra on V2 (The click's algebra beyond the law, The lattice momentum) and the open items 46, 49 and 50 of What is open as the series wrote them. CONTRIBUTING.md states the gates as the rule for the engine.
- **The papers' folder.** The papers stand in `papers/`, one folder each: the first paper moved from `paper/` to `papers/one_rule/`, its content unchanged byte for byte (a three-line `paper/README.md` points to it; at the tag v1.1.0 the old path stands forever), and the time paper, "A discrete spacetime of events in whole numbers: why clocks slow, why nothing outruns light, and why time has a direction only where something was measured", enters as `papers/spacetime_events/` with its run, its records and the checks its `build.sh` runs; every path in the tests, the tools and the documents follows.
- **The papers' own records.** Each paper is archived as a preprint in a Zenodo record of its own: the first paper with Online Resource 1 under DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23297864 (version 1.1.0, dated 2026-10-06; the concept DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23297863), the time paper under DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23297705 (version 1; the concept DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23297704); `CITATION.cff` cites each record for its paper, and the software's DOIs stay what they are.

## Version 1.1.0 (2026-10-06)

The version of record of the first paper, "Universe24: one simple rule, the formulas it shares with nature and adds", archived on Zenodo (the concept DOI [10.5281/zenodo.23190113](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23190113), the version's DOI [10.5281/zenodo.23202100](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23202100)) and at the tag v1.1.0: the paper and its supplement in `paper/`, the law, the derivations as scripts, the engine the paper reports (the commit 441b2399's engine plus the version line), its 150 tests and the two slits' universe. Unchanged: whoever wants the engine the paper reports runs the tag or the Zenodo archive.
